4.0.0-rc-6","draft":false,"immutable":false,"prerelease":true,"created_at":"2026-07-30T15:06:00Z","updated_at":"2026-07-30T16:29:31Z","published_at":"2026-07-30T15:59:05Z","assets":[],"tarball_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/apache/maven/tarball/maven-4.0.0-rc-6","zipball_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/apache/maven/zipball/maven-4.0.0-rc-6","body":"## Notes\n\nThis new release candidate of Maven 4 is released to get feedback from users.\nMaven 4 has restricted a few things compared to Maven 3, so make sure to run the [`mvnup`](https://maven.apache.org/tools/mvnup.html#Plugin_Compatibility_.26_Upgrades) tool before trying your project with Maven 4.\n\n### Issues fixed since RC-5\n\nAll known issues reported in the RC-5 release notes have been fixed:\n\n- **Bean configuration bug** — field accessibility state was cached globally, causing plugin configuration injection failures. Fixed in [#11433](https://github.com/apache/maven/pull/11433).\n- **Concurrency issue in the v4 API** — a `ConcurrentModificationException` was fixed in [#11429](https://github.com/apache/maven/pull/11429).\n- **BOM packaging** — consumer POM conversion for BOM projects now correctly preserves dependency versions. Fixed in [#11464](https://github.com/apache/maven/pull/11464).\n- **macOS Gatekeeper** — the extracted JLine native binaries that triggered Gatekeeper have been removed from the distribution ([#11997](https://github.com/apache/maven/pull/11997)). The `xattr` workaround from RC-5 is no longer needed.\n\n### Known compatibility issues\n\nThe following are known compatibility issues when using Maven 4. Running\n[`mvnup`](https://maven.apache.org/tools/mvnup.html) before building with\nMaven 4 will fix many common plugin version issues automatically. The issues\nbelow are those that require manual intervention or upstream fixes.\n\n#### Stricter POM validation\n\nMaven 4 enforces stricter validation than Maven 3. Projects may need to fix their POMs:\n\n- **Duplicate XML elements** — duplicate ``, ``, etc. are now rejected (`Duplicated tag` error). Fix the source POM.\n- **Duplicate dependency declarations** — same `groupId:artifactId` declared twice is now rejected (`must be unique`).\n- **Uninterpolated expressions** — `${...}` expressions in repository URLs, distribution management IDs, and other fields that Maven 3 silently accepted are now rejected.\n- **Invalid XML in transitive POMs** — illegal processing instructions or undeclared namespace prefixes in transitive POMs cause `Non-parseable POM` errors.\n- **Properties in `` paths** — Maven 4 no longer interpolates properties like `${spark.version}` in `` elements at POM reading time.\n- **CI-friendly versions** — projects using `${revision}` without `flatten-maven-plugin` may hit missing dependency version errors.\n- **Invalid Collect Request** — transitive dependencies with uninterpolated `${...}` version expressions are rejected.\n\n#### Transitive dependency resolution changes\n\nMaven 4's `TransitiveDependencyManager` applies `dependencyManagement` at all transitive depths (Maven 3 only applied it at the first level). This is intentional but can change resolved versions:\n\n- Dependencies previously on the classpath may disappear — add explicit declarations. See [#12302](https://github.com/apache/maven/issues/12302).\n- `maven-enforcer-plugin` version rules may trigger due to downgraded transitive versions.\n- `license-maven-plugin` may report new transitive dependencies not in the allow-list.\n- Karaf feature verification may fail due to OSGi bundle version mismatches.\n\n#### Plugin and extension compatibility\n\nSome plugins and extensions require specific versions for Maven 4:\n\n- **Quarkus** — versions before 3.20 use `ServiceLocator`/`RepositorySystem` patterns incompatible with Maven 4's Sisu-based DI. Upgrade to Quarkus 3.20+.\n- **Tycho** — versions before 5.0 use Guice/Sisu injection patterns broken by Maven 4. Upgrade to Tycho 5.0.3+.\n- **Develocity Maven extension** — fails to initialize due to SLF4J classloading changes (`ClassNotFoundException: SimpleLogger`).\n- **pgpverify-maven-plugin** — versions before 1.20 hit a `ClassCastException` with Maven 4's resolver. See [resolver#1957](https://github.com/apache/maven-resolver/pull/1957).\n- **cyclonedx-maven-plugin** — fails with `Invalid Version Range Request` when using `${revision}` CI-friendly versions.\n- **maven-site-plugin** — Velocity template rendering may fail with incompatible site plugin versions.\n- **maven-shade-plugin** — `dependency-reduced-pom.xml` can cause `The parents form a cycle` error.\n- **commons-release-plugin** — `clean-staging` fails under Maven 4.\n\n#### Classloading changes\n\n- Plugins relying on Maven 3's classrealm layout may encounter **\"foreign imports\"** errors (e.g., Quarkus, JAXB plugins).\n- Maven 4's prefix-based repository routing may block artifacts not matching `/.meta/prefixes.txt`.\n\n#### Post-mvnup formatting\n\n- **spotless-maven-plugin** and **sortpom-maven-plugin** may report formatting violations after `mvnup` modifies POM files. Re-run the formatter after `mvnup`.\n\n#### Other\n\n- **Broken upstream POMs** — e.g., `hadoop-project` 3.1.0–3.3.0 has invalid XML (``) causing parse errors. These need upstream fixes tracked in [HADOOP-19923](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/HADOOP-19923).\n- **Enforcer POM element ordering** — Maven 4's model builder reorders POM elements into a canonical order, which can break enforcer rules checking element ordering.\n- **SNAPSHOT handling** — SNAPSHOT artifacts may show as \"present, but unavailable\" under Maven 4's different resolver metadata handling.\n\n\n## 🚀 New features and improvements\n\n- Backport the use of hardlink instead of file copy ([#11564](https://github.com/apache/maven/pull/11564)) @desruisseaux\n- Accept Java module names as attached artifactId even if they differ from the project's artifactId ([#11573](https://github.com/apache/maven/pull/11573)) @desruisseaux\n- Add module-aware resource handling for :07:44Z","assets":[],"tarball_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/apache/maven/tarball/maven-4.0.0-rc-5","zipball_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/apache/maven/zipball/maven-4.0.0-rc-5","body":"\r\n\r\n## Notes\r\n\r\nThis new release candidate of Maven 4 is released to get feedback from users.\r\nMaven 4 has a restrained a few things comparent to Maven 3, so make sure to run the [`mvnup`](https://maven.apache.org/tools/mvnup.html#Plugin_Compatibility_.26_Upgrades) tool before trying to project with Maven 4.\r\n\r\n### Bean configuration bug\r\n\r\nA bug has been found in the bean configuration system where field accessibility state is cached globally. This can cause plugin configuration injection to fail when the same configuration field is accessed multiple times or in different contexts during a build. This particularly affects the plugin unit tests.\r\n\r\nThis will be fixed by #11433 in the next release.\r\n\r\n### Concurrency issue in the v4 API\r\n\r\nA concurrency issue has been found in the Maven 4 API (still in preview mode) and will be fixed by #11428 in the next release.\r\n\r\n### BOM packaging\r\n\r\nAnother bug has been found in how BOM projects are processed. When a project uses BOM packaging, the consumer POM is not being properly converted to standard POM packaging, and dependency versions could be lost in some cases.\r\n\r\nThis will be fixed by #11427 in the next release.\r\n\r\n### macOS: JLine native library may be blocked by Gatekeeper on first use\r\n\r\nOn macOS (especially Apple Silicon), the first invocation of mvn may fail to load the JLine native terminal library with an error such as:\r\n```\r\njava.lang.UnsatisfiedLinkError: .../libjline-native/Mac/arm64/libjlinenative.jnilib: dlopen(...): code signature ... not valid for use in process: library load disallowed by system policy\r\n```\r\nThis occurs when the binary distribution is downloaded via a web browser, which applies the `com.apple.quarantine extended` attribute.\r\n#### Workaround (one-time fix):\r\n```bash\r\nxattr -r -d com.apple.quarantine /path/to/apache-maven-4.0.0-rc-5/lib/jline-native\r\n```\r\nRecommended download method (avoids the issue entirely):\r\n```bash\r\ncurl -L -O https://archive.apache.org/dist/maven/maven-4/4.0.0-rc-5/binaries/apache-maven-4.0.0-rc-5-bin.tar.gz\r\ntar -xzf apache-maven-4.0.0-rc-5-bin.tar.gz\r\n```\r\nThis is a known issue #10747 and will be addressed in a future release.\r\n\r\n## :boom: Breaking changes\r\n\r\n- Disable consumer POM flattening by default and add an opt-in feature (#11347) ([#11370](https://github.com/apache/maven/pull/11370)) @gnodet\r\n\r\n## 🚀 New features and improvements\r\n\r\n- Disable consumer POM flattening by default and add an opt-in feature (#11347) Release notes\r\n\r\nMaven has entered the release candidate phase and aims to be finally released in a few weeks. Please give it a try and report errors.\r\n\r\n# Upgrading from Maven 3\r\n\r\nMaven 4 brings a tons of changes. We've tried hard to maximise compatibility with Maven 3.x, but in order to have your build work with Maven 4, you will need to upgrade some plugins (such as `maven-enforcer-plugin`, `maven-remote-resources-plugin`, `maven-shade-plugin`, etc...) to their most recent versions.\r\n\r\nIf your build is leveraging Maven extensions, you may very well expect some breakage. Some extensions may need to be updated:\r\n* the useful, but unmaintained, [`os-maven-plugin`](https://github.com/trustin/os-maven-plugin/) extension has been replaced with at [`nisse`](https://github.com/maveniverse/nisse) extension.\r\n\r\nContact the extensions' developers team to know their plans regarding supporting to Maven 4.\r\n\r\n# Upgrading from Maven 4.0.0-rc-2\r\n\r\n**Important note about breaking changes:** Maven 4.0.0-rc-3 includes changes in the new API that will require updates in plugin targeting the new API, especially relating to [MNG-8395] which adds a new `` element to the model and redirects the `` element. Plugin developers will need to update their code to work with these changes.\r\n\r\n# Change log\r\n\r\n## What's Changed\r\n* [MNG-8437] mvnsh by @cstamas in Release notes\r\n\r\nMaven has entered the release candidate phase and aims to be finally released in a few weeks. Please give it a try and report errors.\r\n\r\n# Upgrading from Maven 3\r\n\r\nMaven 4 brings a tons of changes. We've tried hard to maximise compatibility with Maven 3.x, but in order to have your build work with Maven 4, you will need to upgrade some plugins (such as `maven-enforcer-plugin`, `maven-remote-resources-plugin`, `maven-shade-plugin`, etc...) to their most recent versions.\r\n\r\nIf your build is leveraging Maven extensions, you may very well expect some breakage. Some extensions may need to be updated:\r\n* the useful, but unmaintained, [`os-maven-plugin`](https://github.com/trustin/os-maven-plugin/) extension has been forked and now maintained at [`os-detector-maven-plugin`](https://github.com/tisonkun/os-detector)\r\n\r\nContact the extensions' developers team to know their plans regarding supporting to Maven 4.\r\n\r\n## Known aven-3.9.1","zipball_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/apache/maven/zipball/maven-3.9.1","body":"[Release Notes - Maven - Version 3.9.1\r\n](https://maven.apache.org/docs/3.9.1/release-notes.html)\r\n--\r\n\r\n\r\nOverview About the Changes\r\n---\r\nRegression fixes from Maven 3.9.0. General performance and other fixes.\r\n\r\nPotentially Breaking Core Changes (if migrating from 3.8.x)\r\n--\r\n- The Maven Resolver transport has changed from Wagon to “native HTTP”, see [Resolver Transport guide](https://maven.apache.org/guides/mini/guide-resolver-transport.html).\r\n\r\n- Maven 2.x was auto-injecting an ancient version of plexus-utils dependency into the plugin classpath, and Maven 3.x continued doing this to preserve backward compatibility. Starting with Maven 3.9, it does not happen anymore. This change may lead to plugin breakage. The fix for affected plugin maintainers is to explicitly declare a dependency on plexus-utils. The workaround for affected plugin users is to add this dependency to plugin dependencies until issue is fixed by the affected plugin maintainer. See [MNG-6965](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/MNG-6965).\r\n- Mojos are prevented to boostrap new instance of RepositorySystem (for example by using deprecated ServiceLocator), they should reuse RepositorySystem instance provided by Maven instead. See [MNG-7471](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/MNG-7471).\r\n- Each line in .mvn/maven.config is now interpreted as a single argument. That is, if the file contains multiple arguments, these must now be placed on separate lines, see [MNG-7684](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/MNG-7684).\r\n\r\nBug\r\n---\r\n\r\n* \\[[MNG-7213](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/MNG-7213)\\] - StackOverflowError when version ranges are unsolvable and graph contains a cycle\r\n* \\[[MNG-7544](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/MNG-7544)\\] - MavenMetadataSource#retrieve(MetadataResolutionRequest) does not check for null when reading from project map\r\n* \\[[MNG-7676](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/MNG-7676)\\] - On Maven 3.9.0+ release, sha512 hashes have \"null\" classifier\r\n* \\[[MNG-7677](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/MNG-7677)\\] - Maven 3.9.0 is ~10% slower than 3.8.7 in large multi-module builds\r\n* \\[[MNG-7679](https://issues.apache.org/jira/browse/MNG-7679)\\] - \\[REGRESSION\\] Build fails when executing a single mojo
